**Session 2026-05-17 (B44.10, +1, 8789 → 8790 — flips `requireOfJsonFileWithoutExtensionResolvesToTs_ts` JS-emit).** Two-piece JSON re-emit fix in `TypeScriptCompiler.kt`:
  - (a) Pre-scan for `.json` imports (`require('./x.json')` or `from './x.json'`). Builds `importedJsonBaseNames` and `jsonBaseNameToImporter`. Only re-emit JSON files in the set when `@resolveJsonModule` is on. Matches TypeScript: unreferenced JSON fixtures like `b.json` in a test that only imports `c.json` are NOT re-emitted.
  - (b) Interleave JSON outputs with JS outputs: each imported JSON appears RIGHT BEFORE the JS output of the importing TS file. Required for `out/c.js, out/c.json, out/file1.js` order where file1.ts imports both c.ts and c.json. Unimported JSON outputs fall back to the start (legacy behavior, preserved for `requireOfJsonFileTypes_ts` which has JSON-only imports).

  **Initial attempt regression.** First tried gating JSON re-emit on `@declaration: true` — regressed 25 tests. The correct condition is "is the JSON file explicitly imported" (matched via regex on raw source).

**Session 2026-05-17 (B44.9, +2, 8787 → 8789 — flips `fileReferencesWithNoExtensions_ts` + `jsFileCompilationErrorOnDeclarationsWithJsFileReferenceWithOutDir_ts` JS-emit).** Enable `/// <reference>` dep edges universally (was outFile-only) with cycle-fallback. Two-piece:
  - (a) `includeReferencePathDeps = true` always. Extended ref-path resolver to try `.ts`/`.tsx`/`.d.ts` extensions when the specifier omits them (e.g. `<reference path="a"/>`).
  - (b) New `hasCycle` helper (3-color DFS). When the full deps graph (with ref-path edges) has any cycle, fall back to the deps map WITHOUT ref-path edges (just import-based deps). Required to keep `doNotemitTripleSlashComments_ts` and `doNotEmitTripleSlashCommentsOnNotEmittedNode_ts` passing — those have 3-way mutual `<reference>` cycles between files; TypeScript emits in input order in that case.

  **Previous attempts (B42.4 era) regressed on the cycle cases.** The B44.9 fix is the same idea but with explicit cycle detection rather than dep-edge filtering. The two computed deps maps (with and without ref-path) are both built in the parsing loop; selection between them is post-loop based on cycle check on the full deps.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B44.7, +3, 8781 → 8784 — flips `pathMappingBasedModuleResolution{6_node, 7_classic, 7_node}_ts` JS-emit).** Implement rootDirs virtual file merging in `extractRelativeImports`. New `rootDirs: List<String>?` parameter threaded from `options.rootDirs`. Algorithm: identify the importing file's actual rootDir (longest-prefix match), then for relative specifiers that didn't resolve against the file's directory, try each ALTERNATE rootDir by swapping the file's prefix. Resolves `c:/root/src/file1.ts`'s `./project/file2` to `c:/root/generated/src/project/file2.ts` when `rootDirs: [".", "../generated/src"]`. Probes `.ts/.tsx/.mts/.cts` + `/index.*` variants. **Session 2026-05-17 (B44.2, +1, 8773 → 8774 — flips `requireOfJsonFileTypes_ts` JS-emit).** Continuation of session after B44.1. Picked from JS-emit ranker (16-line diff). Normalize whitespace (`,` → `, `, `:` → `: `, drop runs of whitespace) and return on one line, preserving quoted-string spans verbatim (with `\\` escape handling). **Session 2026-05-17 (B44.1, +1, 8772 → 8773 — flips `inferTypePredicates_ts` JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B43.4. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0; surveyed JS-emit ranker for previously-unclassified candidates. Picked `inferTypePredicates_ts` (13-line diff) — source contains multiple `// should error` / `// should ok` line comments between `=` and a multi-line array-method-chain initializer, plus comments between `arr` and `.filter` in arrow-body chained calls.

  **Two-piece fix.** Both shapes require capturing same-line `// line comment` that the scanner classifies as "trailing of the previous token" but where the next non-trivia token is on a new line. `scanner.getTrailingComments()` returns these immediately after the parse step that consumed the relevant token; they are lost on the next scan.

  - **(a) `VariableDeclaration.initializerLeadingTrailingComments`** (new optional field). In `parseVariableDeclaration`, after `parseOptional(SyntaxKind.Equals)` returns true, capture `scanner.getTrailingComments()` filtered to `//` comments with `hasTrailingNewLine` AND `!hasPrecedingNewLine`, gated on `scanner.hasPrecedingLineBreak()` (initializer is on next line). Emitter consumes via early-return branch: writes `= <comment text>\n <value>` (newline + single space, matching TypeScript's emit shape). Skips the existing `findInlineInitializerOwnLineComments` / `findInlineInitializerComment` paths since these handle the `hasPrecedingNewLine=true` and inline-after-`=` shapes, respectively.
  - **(b) `PropertyAccessExpression.expressionTrailingLineComments`** (new optional field). In the `Dot` branch of `parseCallAndAccess`, BEFORE `nextToken()` consumes the dot, capture `scanner.getTrailingComments()` filtered the same way. Gated on `newLineBefore` (the dot follows a newline) AND `result.trailingComments.isNullOrEmpty()` — without this second gate, `CallExpression` (which captures `callTrailing` when next token is Dot/OpenBracket at line 4264) would double-emit the comment. Without the gate: `var r1 = v1.func(...) .func(...) // error\n .func(...)` produces `// error // error` (duplicated). Emitter emits as ` <comment>` after the expression's own trailingComments but before the newline-indent-dot block.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1302/3 (was 10078/1303/3, +1 net). Zero regressions. Initial attempt (without the `result.trailingComments` gate in piece (b)) regressed 2 tests (`genericChainedCalls_ts`, `overEagerReturnTypeSpecialization_ts`) with duplicate `// error` comments — those tests' CallExpression-then-`.next` chain already routes the comment through CallExpression's `callTrailing`. Gating on emptiness of `result.trailingComments` resolves both regressions cleanly.

  **CLAUDE.md gotcha to add.** Document the trailing-line-comments-between-newlines pattern: when a `// comment` ends a line and the next significant token is on a new line, scanner puts the comment in `trailingComments` (not `leadingComments`) because `seenLineBreak` was false when the comment started. Parsers that consume comments via `scanner.getTrailingComments()` immediately after the relevant `nextToken()` can capture them; otherwise they are lost on the next scan.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B42.1 + B42.2 + B42.3 + B42.4 + B42.5 + B42.6, +6 net, 8763 → 8769 passing).** Continuation /loop iteration after B41.2. Six surgical fixes landed in one session — three in `@isolatedModules` cross-file analysis (B42.1/B42.2/B42.3), one in multi-file dependency-ordering for bare specifiers (B42.4), two in parser error-recovery wording (B42.5 TS1247-vs-TS1246, B42.6 TS2809 destructuring-assignment hint).

**B42.6 (+1, flips `destructionAssignmentError_ts`).** Added TS2809 detection in `parsePrimaryExpression`'s else branch: when the current token is `Equals` and the immediately-preceding non-trivia character (via direct source-text scan from `scanner.getTokenPos()`) is `}`, emit TS2809 with the destructuring-assignment hint instead of generic TS1109. Other paths to TS1109 (template-literal EOF, yield-as-type-assertion-arg, etc.) are unaffected.

**B42.5 (+1, flips `errorOnInitializerInObjectTypeLiteralProperty_ts`).** Parser's `parseTypeMember` (shared by interface bodies and type literals) now picks TS1247 ("A type literal property cannot have an initializer.") vs TS1246 ("An interface property cannot have an initializer.") based on a new class-level flag `inTypeLiteralForErrorWording` toggled by `parseTypeLiteralOrMappedType` with try/finally restore.

**B42.4 (+1, flips `requireOfJsonFileNonRelativeWithoutExtensionResolvesToTs_ts` JS-emit).** `extractRelativeImports` now walks up from the current file's directory looking for `node_modules/<specifier>.ts` / `.tsx` / `.d.ts` / `/index.{ts,tsx,d.ts}` when a bare specifier didn't resolve via the standard candidate list. Probe-dir walk: start at `dir`, try probes; on no match move up one segment via `lastIndexOf('/')` and retry; stop at empty string. Only fires for non-relative specifiers AFTER standard candidates failed — bounded fallback. Multi-file test fixtures that set up `@Filename: /src/node_modules/X.ts` and import via bare specifier from a sibling rely on this dep edge for `topologicalSort` to produce the correct emit order (node_modules/X.js before the importer).

**Attempted but reverted: reference-path deps for non-outFile.** Tried enabling `includeReferencePathDeps = true` for all multi-file modes (previously gated on `options.outFile != null`). Plus a cycle-detection refinement that drops dep edges when the target's reference paths point back to the source file. Result: 1 flip (`jsFileCompilationErrorOnDeclarationsWithJsFileReferenceWithOutDir_ts`) but 1 regression (`doNotemitTripleSlashComments_ts` — triangle cycle file0→file2→file1→file0 isn't caught by direct-back-reference check, indirect cycles still mess up post-order DFS). Net 0. Reverted. Future work: cycle detection that handles transitive cycles (proper SCC algorithm) before this can land.

**B42.3 (+1, flips `isolatedModulesExportImportUninstantiatedNamespace_ts` errors-baseline).** Added TS1269 emission: "Cannot use 'export import' on a type or type-only namespace when 'isolatedModules' is enabled." Fires for `export import X = Y` where Y resolves to a type-only entity. Detection extends `isExportedNameTypeOnly` semantics inline to recognize `export namespace` with `ModuleInstanceState.NonInstantiated` (the existing helper only handled TypeAlias/Interface/Class/Function/Enum/VariableStatement). Skips `ExternalModuleReference` (the `export import X = require(...)` case — different diagnostic family). Squiggle: walks backward from `stmt.pos` (= `import` keyword position) to find the preceding `export` modifier; ends at `;`.

**Attempted but reverted (TS1205 for `export { X }` type-only specs).** Added a `checkIsolatedModulesReExportType` pass for TS1205 "Re-exporting a type when 'isolatedModules' is enabled requires using 'export type'." Pattern: `export { X } from "./mod"` where X is type-only, OR `export { X };` where X is a local type-only entity. **Net 0** — the only test that needed JUST TS1205 is `isolatedModulesReExportType_ts`, but that test needs FOUR diagnostics (TS1205×2 + TS1269 for export-import-require + TS1448 for type-only-re-export-chain) to flip. My TS1205 implementation also fired incorrectly for `export { TT }` (TT from `import type`) and `export { CC }` (TS1448 case). Reverted — partial implementation produces no net change and risks breaking other tests with different `import type` semantics. Future work: implement TS1448 (re-export-of-type-only-chain) + TS1269 for export-import-require + tighten TS1205 to skip `import type` locals.

**B42.1 (+1, flips `isolatedModulesExportDeclarationType_ts` JS-emit).** Cross-file `import { T } from "./type"` where T resolves to a type-only export was incorrectly leaving the `const type_1 = require(...)` + `exports.default = type_1.T` emit in place. Root cause: `isValueExport("T", "./type", ...)` returned true because the T symbol's flags had been polluted by `mergeSymbolTable` (per existing CLAUDE.md "ALL file locals merged into globals at Checker init" gotcha). The polluted T accumulated BlockScopedVariable|Alias|TypeAlias|ExportValue from same-name imports in other files. Fix: `isValueExport` now scans the target file's source statements DIRECTLY to classify declarations of `name`, bypassing the polluted symbol. Companion change: ExportAssignment `export default` now captures and propagates `trailingComments` so `// Ok` comment survives the transformation. Restricted parser change to `export default` only (not `export =`) — under ES-module emission the latter is silently dropped and trailing comments would back up onto the previous statement (regressed `es6ExportAssignment2_ts` initially).

**B42.2 (+1, flips `isolatedModulesAmbientConstEnum_ts` errors-baseline).** Added TS2748 emission: "Cannot access ambient const enums when 'isolatedModules' is enabled" fires for `E.X` where E is a `declare const enum` declaration in a non-.d.ts file. Gate: options.isolatedModules && !options.preserveConstEnums, receiver is Identifier, target symbol declarations include EnumDeclaration with both Const + Declare modifiers. Squiggle at receiver position, length = receiver text. Net +1 zero regressions.

**Survey of remaining isolatedModules-related failures.** Many require multi-piece diagnostic additions: TS18055/TS1281/TS1280 (cross-file enum value source under isolatedModules), TS1205/TS1269/TS1448 (type-only re-export diagnostics for isolatedModulesReExportType_ts), TS2866 (import-conflicts-with-global), TS2865 (import-conflicts-with-local-value). The isolatedModulesGlobalNamespacesAndEnums_ts JS-emit baseline also needs cross-file const-value inlining infrastructure (`d` → `"d"` from another file). All out of scope for surgical work.

**Other candidates surveyed and confirmed architectural.** `asyncFunctionReturnExpressionErrorSpans_ts` (needs Promise<T> unwrap for nested elaboration, not just top-level checkTypeRelatedTo — attempted, reverted; the elaboration needs to thread the unwrapped Foo through the property chain). `discriminatedUnionErrorMessage_ts` / `unionErrorMessageOnMatchingDiscriminant_ts` (discriminated union narrowing in assignment context). `varianceAnnotationValidation_ts` (TS2636 variance annotations — new feature). `signatureCombiningRestParameters1_ts` (intersection contextual typing for arrow params).

**Session 2026-05-17 (B41.2, +1, 8762 → 8763 passing — flips `numericLiteralsWithTrailingDecimalPoints01_ts` JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after post-B41.1 recon. Re-investigated the comment-preservation case after initial attempts failed; the root cause was deeper than expected.

  **Root cause.** For `expr\n  /* comment */ .toString()` shape, the scanner captures `/* comment */` as `leadingComments` on the `.` token. The Parser then calls `nextToken()` to consume `.`. On the NEXT `scanner.scan()` call (for the property name `toString`), the scanner resets `leadingComments = null` (Scanner.kt:253), losing the comments. The `parseIdentifierName()` for `toString` sees empty leading comments. Subsequent emitter logic in `emitPropertyAccess` had no way to find the comment.

  **Fix.** Two-piece:
  - **Parser** (`Parser.kt:parseCallAndAccess`, Dot branch): capture `leadingComments()` BEFORE `nextToken()` consumes the dot (when `newLineBefore=true`); merge them into the property name's `leadingComments` via `.copy(leadingComments=...)`. Only fires for newLineBefore=true to preserve existing semantics for inline `point. /*2*/ x` shape.
  - **Emitter** (`Emitter.kt:emitPropertyAccess`): when `newLineBefore=true` and `name.leadingComments` is non-empty, emit comments AFTER indent / BEFORE dot. Block comments (`MultiLineComment` kind) followed by space; line comments (`SingleLineComment` kind) followed by newline + indent (line comment terminates the line, dot belongs on next indented line). The existing inline-after-dot emission is suppressed when `newLineBefore=true` to avoid double-emit.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1312/3 (was 10078/1313/3, +1 net). Zero regressions. The target test `numericLiteralsWithTrailingDecimalPoints01_ts` JS-emit sub-test flips clean. The errors-baseline sub-test still fails on a separate gap (TS1351 "Identifier or keyword cannot immediately follow numeric literal" not emitted for `1.toString()` / `2.toString()` patterns; unrelated to this fix). Both test12 (`3\n  /* comment */ .toString()`) and test13 (`3.\n  /* comment */ .toString()`) and test14/15 (line-comment variants) now emit correctly.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B41.1, +2, 8760 → 8762 passing — flips `functionsMissingReturnStatementsAndExpressions_ts` target_es5 and target_es2015).** Continuation /loop iteration after B40.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3/62/12). With XMLs still fresh from B40.1, surveyed the non-fresh MISSING DIAGS pool of 62 candidates. Found `functionsMissingReturnStatementsAndExpressions_ts` (MISS TS2355 @ 107,17) had a tractable root cause.

  **Root cause.** The "nullable" classification in `checkBodyForImplicitReturn` (Checker.kt:36758) suppressed TS2355 entirely via a bare `return` early-exit: `if (retTypeClass == "nullable" && !hasAnyReturn) return`. The "nullable" classification matched any TYPE position where `getTypeNodeName` returned "undefined" but `retType` was not a `KeywordTypeNode` — i.e. UnionType containing `undefined`. But TypeScript's actual rule is narrower: `undefined` in a union does NOT satisfy "must return a value" — only `void`/`any`/`never` (in a union) or `undefined` (as bare keyword) suppress TS2355.

  **Fix.** Replaced the bare early-return with a TS2355 emission for "nullable + !hasAnyReturn". Also updated the "pure-undefined" classification to also accept `Promise<undefined>` (where the Promise's single type argument is a `KeywordTypeNode` for `undefined`) — for async functions, `Promise<undefined>` is satisfied by implicit undefined return. The classification split:
  - `KeywordTypeNode(undefined)` → "pure-undefined" (suppressed)
  - `TypeReference(Promise<KeywordTypeNode(undefined)>)` for async → "pure-undefined" (suppressed)
  - Other `undefined`-containing types (UnionType, `Promise<UnionType>`) → "nullable" (TS2355 fires when no returns)

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1313/3 (was 10078/1315/3, +2 net). Zero regressions. Both target_es5 and target_es2015 variants of `functionsMissingReturnStatementsAndExpressions_ts` flip clean. The strict variant (`functionsMissingReturnStatementsAndExpressionsStrictNullChecks_ts`) was already failing on a separate TS2345 case (`f(h1)` arg passing) — my fix correctly adds 4 new TS2355 diagnostics that match baseline but the test remains failing on the unrelated TS2345 gap; net effect on that test: still failing but closer (3 missed → 1 missed).

  **CLAUDE.md gotcha update.** Added a note about how `undefined`-in-union does NOT satisfy "must return a value" — only bare `undefined` keyword (or `Promise<undefined>` for async) does. Mirrors TypeScript's narrower rule.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B40.1, +1, 8759 → 8760 passing — flips `declarationEmitResolveTypesIfNotReusable_ts` JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B39.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3/62/12). Re-ran the JS-emit ranker (`/tmp/find_js_emit_candidates.py`) — 159 candidates. Spotted `declarationEmitResolveTypesIfNotReusable_ts` (3-line diff) — source contains `(o: typeof a['a']) => {}` and we parse it as `(o, []) => 'a'; { }` (the `['a']` is misinterpreted as a destructured second parameter).

  **Root cause.** Parser's `TypeOfKeyword` branch in `parseNonArrayType` (Parser.kt:6109-6127) handles array suffix `typeof X[]` but does NOT handle indexed-access suffix `typeof X[K]`. So `typeof a['a']` parses as `typeof a` (returning early at the bracket-with-non-`]`), leaving `['a']` for the outer parameter-parser to misinterpret. The non-TypeOf branch (a few lines below) DOES handle `T[K]` via the same `while (token == OpenBracket)` loop.

  **Fix.** Extended the TypeOf branch's loop to handle the indexed-access case via the existing pattern: when bracket is not empty, consume `[`, parse an index type, expect `]`, wrap in `IndexedAccessType`. Also added `!scanner.hasPrecedingLineBreak()` ASI guard to match the primary-type loop's behavior — protects against consuming `[` on a new line.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1315/3 (was 10078/1316/3, +1 net). Zero regressions; only target test flips clean.

  **Risk profile that materialized.** LOW as expected. Existing array-suffix `typeof X[]` and `typeof X<T>[]` cases preserved (still hit the lookahead-empty-bracket arm first). The new arm only fires for non-empty bracket content — adds previously-missing IndexedAccess parsing for `typeof X[K]`. The ASI guard is purely additive (the prior code didn't guard against bracket-on-new-line; the primary-type path already does).

**Session 2026-05-17 (B39.1, +1, 8758 → 8759 passing — flips `exportAssignmentImportMergeNoCrash_ts` JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B38.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3/62/12). Re-ran the JS-emit ranker (`/tmp/find_js_emit_candidates.py`) — 160 candidates. Picked `exportAssignmentImportMergeNoCrash_ts` (4-line diff, CJS module + `import Obj from "./assignment"; export const Obj = void Obj;` — local `Obj` collides with imported `Obj`).

  **Root cause.** The CJS transform emitted `const assignment_1 = __importDefault(require("./assignment"))` as expected, but the post-CJS elision pass (Transformer.kt Step 2, ~line 2486) removed it because `assignment_1` was never referenced in the rewritten output. Why? The local `Obj` (from `export const Obj = ...`) takes the Direct path: `renameMap["Obj"] = exports.Obj`, overriding the import alias's `renameMap["Obj"] = assignment_1.default`. So all `Obj` references in the body become `exports.Obj` and `assignment_1` truly is unused. TypeScript however still emits the require for its side effects, because the imported default binding `Obj` IS referenced in the original source (`void Obj` on the RHS of the const declaration — TDZ-style).

  **Fix.** Added a second exemption to the elision check (Transformer.kt:2486-2547): identify import const stmts whose user-facing DEFAULT-binding name (from `clause.name`) is BOTH (a) shadowed by a same-name top-level `VariableStatement`/`FunctionDeclaration`/`ClassDeclaration`, AND (b) referenced in `valueReferencedNames` (original-source value references). For such stmts, skip elision. Gate is intentionally narrow:
  - **Default imports only** — named-import bindings can resolve to type-only targets (`import { X }` where `X` came via `export type`), and those should still elide.
  - **Shadowed cases only** — without shadow, normal const-enum imports (whose references get inlined to `0 /* X.Foo */`) would be erroneously kept.

  Initial attempt (broader rule: keep any import where user-facing names are referenced) regressed 3 const-enum tests because const enum imports rely on the post-rewriting elision after their references are inlined away. The narrowed shadow-only gate resolves all 3 regressions.

  **Full-suite verification:** 10078/1316/3 (was 10078/1317/3, +1 net). Zero regressions. Only the target test flips.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B38.1, +1, 8757 → 8758 passing — flips `privacyTopLevelInternalReferenceImportWithExport_ts` JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B37.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3/62/12). Re-ran the JS-emit ranker (`/tmp/find_js_emit_candidates.py`) — 161 candidates. Picked `privacyTopLevelInternalReferenceImportWithExport_ts` (4-line diff, AMD module + `namespace m_private { ...; export interface i_private {}; export namespace mu_private { interface i {} } }` + top-level `export import im_public_i_private = m_private.i_private;`).

  **Root cause.** Two-piece divergence: (a) the void0 reset chain included `exports.im_public_i_private` and `exports.im_public_mu_private` where TypeScript skipped them; (b) body assignments `exports.im_public_i_private = m_private.i_private;` and `exports.im_public_mu_private = m_private.mu_private;` were emitted but TypeScript erased them. Both stem from one decision: should the `export import X = m_private.Y` alias be elided when `Y` is type-only but `m_private` is non-exported?

  **Prior gate (`requireRootExported = true`).** Introduced by commit 72bf8de ("fix: suppress CJS void0 hoist and assignment for type-only import aliases", 3 tests). Hypothesis: TypeScript erases `export import b = a.I` only when `a` is itself exported. The hypothesis fit the 3 `internalAlias*` tests at the time but was incomplete — it kept aliases that TypeScript erases when the root is non-exported BUT runtime-instantiated.

  **Distinguishing test cases** (cross-referenced against TS baselines):
  | Case | Root | Target type | TS Output |
  |------|------|-------------|-----------|
  | privacy `m_private.i_private` | NOT exported, runtime | interface | ERASED |
  | privacy `m_private.mu_private` | NOT exported, runtime | type-only ns | ERASED |
  | privacy `m_private.mi_private` | NOT exported, runtime | runtime ns (has class) | KEPT |
  | `internalAliasInterfaceInsideTopLevelModuleWithExport` `a.I` | exported, type-only ns | interface | ERASED |
  | `importDeclWithExportModifier` `x.c` | NOT exported, type-only ns | interface | KEPT (with TS2708/TS2694) |

  Pattern: TypeScript erases when the target sub-member resolves to type-only AND the root is EITHER exported OR runtime-instantiated. The non-exported-non-runtime case keeps a syntactically-correct but runtime-broken emit (errors fire but JS is still produced).

  **Fix.** Renamed parameter `requireRootExported` → `requireRuntimeOrExportedRoot` on `isQualifiedPathTypeOnly`. New gate condition: `if (i == 0 && requireRuntimeOrExportedRoot) { val isExported = ModifierFlag.Export in ns.modifiers; val isRuntime = !isTypeOnlyNamespace(ns); if (!isExported && !isRuntime) return false }`. Three call sites updated (CJS pre-scan, AMD pre-scan, `transformImportEqualsDeclaration`); variable name updated to `requireRuntimeOrExported`. Existing Identifier-target gates preserved (single-segment paths stay exported-only since they cover top-level `import b = a` form).

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1317/3 (was 10078/1318/3, +1 net). Zero regressions. Target test `privacyTopLevelInternalReferenceImportWithExport_ts` flips clean. Previously identified regressed tests (`importDeclWithClassModifiers_ts`, `importDeclWithExportModifier_ts`, `importDeclWithExportModifierAndExportAssignment_ts`) all preserve their KEEP behavior because their roots (`x`, type-only namespace with only `interface c`) are neither exported nor runtime — the new gate correctly returns false. Original 3 `internalAlias*` tests preserved (their roots are `export namespace a` — exported, even if type-only).

  **First-attempt gotcha recorded.** Tried a simpler "always erase regardless of root" approach first (drop the gate entirely). Full-suite -2 net (1318 → 1320 fail): privacy +1, but `importDeclWithClassModifiers_ts`, `importDeclWithExportModifier_ts`, `importDeclWithExportModifierAndExportAssignment_ts` all flipped to fail. Diagnostic: TypeScript distinguishes "alias to type-only via runtime root" (erase) from "alias to type-only via type-only-non-exported root" (keep with runtime-broken emit). The refined gate captures both.

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** Same `requireRuntimeOrExportedRoot` parameter could be useful for any other type-only-path analysis that consumes `isQualifiedPathTypeOnly`. The dual-condition gate (exported OR runtime) reflects TypeScript's emit semantics for ambiguous value-vs-type qualified-name targets. JS-emit ranker pool drops 161 → 160.

  **Landed: System format named re-export per-statement grouping.** Picked `reexportMissingDefault5_ts` (4-line diff). Source:
  ```ts
  // @module: system
  // @filename: a.ts
  export { b } from "./b";
  export { default as Foo } from "./b";
  ```
  Expected: TWO `exports_1({...})` calls (one per source statement). Actual: ONE combined call.

  **Root cause.** `Transformer.kt`'s `pathToNamedReExports` was `Map<String, MutableList<Pair<String, String>>>` — a flat list of (exportName, importedName) per module path. Two consecutive `export {...} from "./b"` statements both appended to the same list, and the setter-body generator at line ~5332 built ONE `ObjectLiteralExpression` from all pairs and emitted ONE `exports_1({...})` call. TypeScript emits one call per source statement, not one combined call per path.

  **Single-piece fix in `Transformer.kt`.** Restructured `pathToNamedReExports` to `Map<String, MutableList<MutableList<Pair<String, String>>>>` (outer-list = per-statement groups). The population site (~line 4626) builds a fresh `group: MutableList<Pair<String, String>>` per `ExportDeclaration` with `NamedExports` clause and module specifier, populates it from `stmt.exportClause.elements`, and appends to the outer list (skipping empty groups). The consumer site (~line 5332) iterates groups, emitting one `ObjectLiteralExpression` + one `exports_1({...})` `ExpressionStatement` per non-empty group.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1322/3 (was 10078/1323/3 in B33.1, +1 net). Zero regressions. Target test flips clean.

  **Risk profile.** Bounded. The only structural change is grouping behavior under `@module: system` when multiple `export {...} from "X"` declarations share the same module specifier. Single-declaration cases collapse to a single group → identical output to the prior flat form. The empty-group skip preserves prior behavior for type-only `export type { ... } from "X"` statements where all specs filter out as type-only. Other `pathToNamedReExports` consumers (membership checks at lines 5240/5250 — `path in pathToNamedReExports` and `path !in pathToNamedReExports`) keep working since the key set is unchanged.

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** JS-emit ranker pool drops 166 → 165. The per-statement grouping pattern (one emit per source declaration) may apply to similar AMD/CJS re-export emit paths if a candidate surfaces. Most remaining JS-emit candidates are deep-parser-recovery (`manyCompilerErrors`, `classMemberWithMissingIdentifier2`, `errorRecoveryInClassDeclaration`, `parserUnparsedTokenCrash2`, JSX `<{`-recovery follow-ups) or architectural-blocker (`enumWithNonLiteralStringInitializer` needs cross-file const-string tracking; `moduleNoneDynamicImport` needs `@module: none` CJS-routing carve-out; `moduleResolutionWithExtensions_withPaths` needs `.js`-emit gate respecting `allowJs`). Surgical pool genuinely thinning.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B33.1, +1, 8751 → 8752 passing — flips `syntheticDefaultExportsWithDynamicImports_ts` JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B32.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3/62/12). Recent commit log healthy (B25.1 → B32.1 stack — no recon-only pattern). Anti-loop rule satisfied. Re-ran the JS-emit ranker (`/tmp/find_js_emit_candidates.py`) — 167 candidates. Picked `syntheticDefaultExportsWithDynamicImports_ts` (3-line diff, System module + `import("package")` dynamic-import).

  **Root cause.** Under `@module: system @target: es6 @moduleResolution: bundler`, the source `import("package").then(({default: foo}) => foo(42));` has two issues: (a) raw `import("package")` left untransformed in the System format `execute` body — TypeScript rewrites it to `context_1.import("package")` using System's runtime context API; (b) Emitter's `hasModuleStatements()` doesn't recognize dynamic imports, so it returned `false` and `emitUseStrict()` emitted `"use strict";` at the top BEFORE `System.register(...)` — the System format already emits `"use strict"` inside its function body, so the top-level emission is duplicate. `Transformer.isModuleFile()` already recognizes dynamic imports via `stmtContainsDynamicImport`/`exprContainsDynamicImport`, but `Emitter.hasModuleStatements()` doesn't — the two helpers had diverged.

  **Two-piece fix.**
  - **Transformer.kt:** Added `buildSystemDynamicImport(spec)` → `CallExpression(PropertyAccess(context_1, import), [spec])`. Added `rewriteSystemDynExpr/Stmt` recursive walkers (mirror of `rewriteCjsDynExpr/Stmt` shape). In `transformToSystem`, after `renamedExecute` is computed, map it through `rewriteSystemDynStmt` to produce `dynRewrittenExecute`. Also rewrite `functionHoists` (function bodies can contain dynamic imports). Updated `finalExecute` derivation to use `dynRewrittenExecute`.
  - **Emitter.kt:** Added `statementContainsDynamicImport`/`expressionContainsDynamicImport` helpers (mirror of Transformer's) and OR'd into `hasModuleStatements()`. Now the Emitter detects dynamic-import-only module files and skips the top-level `"use strict";` for System (line 169 gate already covers this once `hasModuleStatements` returns true).

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1323/3 (was 10078/1324/3 in B32.1, +1 net). Zero regressions. Target test flips clean.

  **Risk profile that materialized.** Bounded. The new System dynamic-import rewrite only fires under `@module: system` + `isModuleFile(sourceFile)` true (which already requires the file to be a module). The Emitter `hasModuleStatements` extension is more impactful in principle — any file with a dynamic `import()` call but no traditional import/export will now be classified as a module — but the cascading effects are: (a) System format skips top-level `"use strict"` (correct); (b) AMD/UMD format does the same (correct — AMD/UMD wraps in `define()` and emits `"use strict"` inside); (c) CommonJS format would similarly skip top-level `"use strict"` (correct — CommonJS transform inserts its own `"use strict"` at top of transformed statements). Pre-existing tests with module-classified files were already going through these paths, so the change is consistent.

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** Same pattern (the divergence between `Transformer.isModuleFile` and `Emitter.hasModuleStatements`) could apply to other module-signal types if they exist; the fix here brings the two helpers into line for dynamic imports. JS-emit ranker pool drops 167 → 166.

Targeted test flipped but full suite went -21 net (1325 → 1346 failed). Reverted. Root cause: TypeScript's program model treats only the LAST `@filename` (or some convention) as the root; our model treats all as roots. Many tests rely on the original `tsFileNames`-iteration order; changing it broke them. The two models diverge in non-trivial ways for triple-slash references and ambient-only files. Architectural fix.

  **Landed: AMD bundle module-name relative to commonSourceDir.** Picked the AMD-specific candidate `declarationEmitBundleWithAmbientReferences_ts` (4-line diff). Expected: `define("datastore_result", ...)` + `define("conditional_directive_field", ...)`. Actual: `define("src/datastore_result", ...)` + `define("src/conditional_directive_field", ...)` (full path retained). Root cause: AMD module names in a bundle (`outFile: out/datastore.bundle.js`) should be relative to the longest common ancestor of bundled source files (`src/`), not retained as full paths.

  **Fix.** Added `amdBundleCommonSourceDir` parameter to `Transformer`. In `TypeScriptCompiler.kt`, compute it as `longestCommonPathPrefix(tsFileNames.map { parentDir(it) })` when `outFile != null && tsFileNames.size > 1` (independent of the existing `commonSourceDir` which is outDir-gated). Pass it to each Transformer invocation. In the AMD `define()` call builder (`Transformer.kt` ~3952), after the existing path-cleanup chain (strip `./`, extension, leading `/`, Windows drive letter), strip the common-dir prefix when the derived name starts with `"$commonDir/"`. Common-dir is null for single-file bundles or when files don't share a subdir — fallback is the original full-path-minus-extension behavior.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1324/3 (was 10078/1325/3 in B31.1, +1 net). Zero regressions. `.d.ts` files are excluded from the common-dir calc (they're not emitted into the bundle), so the ambient `lib/lib.d.ts` doesn't drag commonSourceDir up to `/`.

  **Risk profile.** Bounded. The strip only activates when (a) `outFile != null`, (b) multiple TS files in the bundle, (c) all files share a non-empty subdirectory ancestor, AND (d) the derived AMD module name starts with `$commonDir/`. Single-file bundles, files in the bundle root, or `<amd-module name='...'/>` overrides all bypass the strip.

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** Same common-dir parameter could feed AMD `factoryName` derivation (currently independent) or System-format module-name handling. Pool drops 168 → 167.

Same pattern as B29.1's commonSourceDir family but with a single TS file plus a referenced `.d.ts` sibling.

  **Root cause.** B29.1's `commonSourceDir` calc considered only `tsFileNames` (excluding `.d.ts`). Test fixture has only one TS file `/app/src/index.ts` plus a `.d.ts` reference at `/app/lib/bar.d.ts`. With only `[/app/src/index.ts]` contributing, `longestCommonPathPrefix` returned `/app/src`, so `index.ts → /app/bin/index.js` (relative-from-`/app/src` = `index.js`). TypeScript instead includes `.d.ts` files under the project tree, giving commonSourceDir=`/app` and output `/app/bin/src/index.js`.

  **Why not just include ALL `.d.ts`.** The sibling test `commonSourceDirectory_ts` (currently passing) has `.d.ts` at `/types/bar.d.ts` while sources live at `/app/index.ts` — including ALL `.d.ts` would shift commonSourceDir to `/`, breaking that test. TypeScript's rule: `.d.ts` files outside the project root (typically reached via `typeRoots`, here `typeRoots: ["../types"]`) don't contribute. The proxy: only include `.d.ts` files under the tsconfig directory.

  **Fix.** New `dtsFileNamesInProjectDir` mutable list populated in the existing `if (isDtsFile)` branch (~line 1024) when `!isNodeModulesFile && computedTsconfigDir != null && file.fileName.startsWith("$computedTsconfigDir/")`. `commonSourceDir` calc concatenates `tsFileNames + dtsFileNamesInProjectDir` before computing parent dirs and LCP. Also extracted `computedTsconfigDir` to a single local at the top of the multi-file branch (was previously computed inline twice — once for `resolvedOutDir`, once for `extractRelativeImports`).

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1325/3 (was 10078/1326/3 in B30.1, +1 net). Zero regressions. `commonSourceDirectory_ts` (passing) confirmed still passes; `commonSourceDirectory_dts_ts` flips. Other `.d.ts`-using emit tests unaffected since the gate restricts to project-dir `.d.ts` only.

  **Gotcha worth recording.** `commonSourceDirectory` in TypeScript includes `.d.ts` files under the project root but excludes `.d.ts` files reached via `typeRoots` or `node_modules`. We approximate via the "starts with tsconfigDir + /" check. If a future test sets `typeRoots` to a path INSIDE the project root (e.g. `typeRoots: ["./types"]` from `/app/tsconfig.json` → `/app/types/`), this approximation would over-include and shift commonSourceDir. No current tests trip this; would need actual `typeRoots` parsing to handle.

Re-ran the JS-emit ranker (`/tmp/find_js_emit_candidates.py`) — 171 candidates. Spotted `pathMappingBasedModuleResolution8_node_ts` (4-line diff) — pattern is two-file file-ordering swap: the importer's JS block is emitted before the exporter's JS block. Same diff applied to `_classic` variant.

  **Root cause.** Test fixture has `paths: {"@speedy/*/testing": ["*/dist/index.ts"]}` mapping a non-relative specifier to a path-mapped TS file in a subdir. `extractRelativeImports` (TypeScriptCompiler.kt ~1271) only had two specifier branches: relative (`./` / `../`) and "literal name match" — neither catches a `paths` substitution. So the dep edge from `c:/root/index.ts` to `c:/root/folder1/dist/index.ts` was lost, `topologicalSort` fell back to original file order (importer first), and the two `//// [index.js]` blocks landed swapped vs TypeScript's dependency-first output.

  **Fix.** New helper `resolvePathsMapping(specifier, paths, baseUrl, tsconfigDir, allTsFileNames): String?` in `TypeScriptCompiler.kt` (~1410). Matches the specifier against each `paths` pattern using either literal equality (no `*`) or single-wildcard `*` prefix/suffix matching (captures the wildcard substring). Substitutes the captured wildcard into each substitution string, anchors against `tsconfigDir + baseUrl` (defaulting to tsconfigDir alone when `baseUrl == "."` or unset), and tries known TS extension shapes (`.ts/.tsx/.mts/.cts/.d.ts`), no-extension fallback, and `/index.ts` variants. First candidate that exists in `allTsFileNames` is returned. `extractRelativeImports` now accepts `paths: Map<String, List<String>>`, `baseUrl: String?`, and `tsconfigDir: String?` parameters, and calls the helper BEFORE the existing candidate-list loop for any non-relative specifier when `paths` is non-empty — on match, the dep is added and the loop continues (skipping the standard candidate path).

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1326/3 (was 10078/1328/3 in B29.1, +2 net). Zero regressions. Both `pathMappingBasedModuleResolution8` variants flipped.

  **Risk profile that materialized.** Bounded. The new code path only activates when (a) the specifier is non-relative, AND (b) `options.paths` is non-empty. Most existing tests have empty `paths` so the helper short-circuits at the start of the `extractRelativeImports` block before any new logic runs. For tests with `paths`, the helper at worst falls through to the existing candidate-list behavior (returning null) if no pattern matches or no substituted candidate is in `allTsFileNames`.

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** The same `resolvePathsMapping` helper is now in place for future `paths`-related fixes. Likely tests still affected: any `pathMappingBasedModuleResolution*` numbered variants we haven't yet examined, plus `nodeModulesAtTypesPriority`-shaped tests that rely on path-mapping for dependency ordering. JS-emit ranker pool drops 171 → 169.

Multi-file test sources DO include the `package.json` files (via `// @filename`), but the existing helper had no way to consult them.

  **Fix.**
  - **Added `packageJsonTypes: Map<String, Boolean>` field to `CompilerOptions`**: directory path → `true` for `"type": "module"`, `false` for `"type": "commonjs"`. Default empty.
  - **New helper `collectPackageJsonTypes` (`TypeScriptCompiler.kt` ~1206)**: scans `parsed.files` for `package.json` files (skipping `node_modules/`), regex-extracts `"type": "..."`, and populates the map keyed by the containing directory (preserves leading `/`, empty string for root).
  - **New `isESModuleFormat(options, fileName)` overload (`CompilerOptions.kt` ~240)**: walks the file's directory tree upward (`/a/b/c.ts` → probe `/a/b`, then `/a`, then `""`) and consults `packageJsonTypes` at the nearest enclosing entry. Falls back to the legacy `module + fileName` logic when no enclosing `package.json` is found.
  - **Populated in `TypeScriptCompiler.compile`** (gated on `options.effectiveModule.isNodeNext` — no overhead for non-nodenext compilations).
  - **Migrated all 14 callers** in `Emitter.kt` (2), `Transformer.kt` (5), and `Checker.kt` (4) to the new overload. The legacy `isESModuleFormat(module, fileName)` overload remains for unmigrated call sites (none in the current tree, but kept for callers that don't have `options` in scope).

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1331/3 (was 10078/1341/3 in B26.1, +10 net). The +10 figure suggests the lookup affected many tests with similar `package.json type: "module"` shapes — likely `nodeNextPackageSelfNameWithOutDirDeclDirRootDir` family, `nodeModulesPackageImports`, etc.

  **Pool follow-on.** JS-emit ranker pool dropped from 184 → 174. Remaining low-hanging picks are parser-recovery clusters (skipped per session guidance) or large-diff architectural cases.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B26.1, +1, 8733 → 8734 passing — flips `declarationMapsOutFile_ts` JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B25.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3/62/12). Re-ran the B24.1 JS-emit ranker (`/tmp/find_js_emit_candidates.py`) — 185 JS-emit candidates surfaced. Most 2-4 line diffs are parser-recovery clusters (skipped per session-prompt guidance). `declarationMapsOutFile_ts` (4-line diff) surfaced as a non-parser transformer/emitter candidate: `export { c, Foo }` where `Foo` is from `import {Foo} from "./a"` and `c` is a local `const c = new Foo()` (AMD output). Expected: `Object.defineProperty(exports, "Foo", { ..., get: function () { return a_1.Foo; } })` getter between hoist and body, AND `exports.c = c` IMMEDIATELY after `const c = ...` declaration (not at end of body).

  **Root cause.** The CJS path (`Transformer.kt` ~2059) detects `localName in namedImportLocalNames` and routes through `makeReExportGetter`; the AMD path (line 3629) does not. Additionally, the AMD path appends `exports.X = X` for local-var re-exports at the source position of the `ExportDeclaration` (typically end-of-body), but TypeScript hoists the assignment to immediately after the declaration of `X` so the export becomes available as soon as `X` is bound.

  **Fix in `transformToAMD` (Transformer.kt:3060-3850).**
  - **Track `amdImportLocalNames`** (mirror of CJS `namedImportLocalNames`): for default imports (`import d from "mod"` → adds `d`), named imports (`import { a, b as c }` → adds each `localAlias`), and the combined default+named clause (`import d, ... → adds d`). The named-bindings part of the combined clause is not tracked (matches the existing renameMap gap — out of scope).
  - **Re-export-getter branch in ExportDeclaration**: when `localName in amdImportLocalNames`, look up `renameMap[localName]` (always a `PropertyAccessExpression(mod_1, propName)` for our import shapes), extract `sourceName = mod_1` and `importedProp = propName`, and emit `makeReExportGetter(exportName, sourceName, importedProp, useImportDefault = (importedProp == "default" && esModuleInterop))` into a new `reExportGetters` list. Inserted into `fullBody` between `protectedExportAssignments` and `finalRenamedBody` so the getter appears right after the void0 hoist (matches TypeScript's emit position). The getter body is constructed post-rename and bypasses the `rewriteIdInStatement(renameMap)` step, so `mod_1.Foo` stays as-is (rewriter's `PropertyAccessExpression` case only descends into `.expression`, not `.name`, so the property name `Foo` is naturally safe).
  - **Post-declaration hoist for local-var re-exports**: when `exportName == localName && localName in runtimeDeclaredNames` (i.e. a `export { c }` where `c` is a local `const/let/var/function/class`), queue `makeExportAssignment(exportName, localName)` in a new `pendingLocalExportAssignments: Map<String, Statement>`. After the renaming + elision passes produce `elidedBody`, a new post-pass walks each statement; when it encounters a `VariableStatement` whose `decl.name` includes `c` (or a `FunctionDeclaration`/`ClassDeclaration` with `name.text == "c"`), appends the pending assignment to the result list immediately after that statement and marks `c` as emitted. Unmatched pending entries (e.g. names declared in an ambient `declare var` outside the body) fall through to end-of-body append.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1341/3 (was 10078/1342/3 in B25.1, +1 net). Zero regressions. Pre-existing `es6ExportClauseWithAssignmentInEs5_ts__target_es2015` failure (unrelated CJS compound-assignment emit) remained unchanged.

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** The `pendingLocalExportAssignments` mechanism only applies to the AMD path. The CJS path appends `exports.c = c` at end-of-body for the same shape (`export { c }` of a local-declared var), which doesn't match TypeScript's post-declaration emit — if a CJS test surfaces this pattern in the JS-emit ranker, port the same `pendingLocalExportAssignments` post-pass into `transformToCommonJS`. The `amdImportLocalNames`/`reExportGetters` pair also applies cleanly to UMD (which shares the AMD transform body) — already covered. Possible additional flip targets to check next session: any AMD/UMD test with `export { X }` where X is from `import X from`/`import {X}` AND a local-var re-export.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B24.1, +1, 8731 → 8732 passing — flips `bindingPatternOmittedExpressionNesting_ts` JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B23.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3/62/12). Recent commit log healthy (B17.x → B23.1 stack — no recon-only pattern). Anti-loop rule satisfied. Re-ran the JS-emit ranker (`/tmp/find_js_emit_candidates.py`) — top 4-line-diff JS-emit-only candidate was `bindingPatternOmittedExpressionNesting_ts` (TypeScript emits chained `_a = init, _b = _a[2], _c = _b[1], _d = _b[3]` for nested empty array bindings; we were emitting only `_a = init`).

  **Root cause.** Source: `export let [,,[,[],,[]]] = undefined as any;`. The top-level `ArrayBindingPattern` has all-omitted bound names but contains a nested `ArrayBindingPattern` at index 2, which itself contains two nested empty `[]` `ArrayBindingPattern`s at indices 1 and 3. TypeScript emits side-effect index accesses for every nested array pattern — even empty leaves — to preserve runtime indexing semantics. Our previous behavior: the `allEmptyBindings` branch (Transformer.kt:1421) emitted a single `_a = decl.initializer;` and discarded all nested patterns. Two failures: (a) missing `_b/_c/_d` index-access chain, and (b) the `sideEffectTempVars` hoist emitted four separate `var _a; var _b; var _c; var _d;` statements instead of one `var _a, _b, _c, _d;`.

  **Two-piece fix in Transformer.kt.**
  - **`allEmptyBindings` branch (~1425):** added a recursive `walkArrayPattern(pat, parentTemp)` helper that walks `pat.elements`; for each `BindingElement` whose `.name is ArrayBindingPattern`, allocates a new temp via `nextTempVarName()`, appends `BinaryExpression(syntheticId(nestedTemp), Equals, ElementAccessExpression(parentTemp, i))` to a per-decl `assigns` list, and recurses with the new temp as parent. The full `assigns` list is then folded with comma operator into a single `ExpressionStatement`.
  - **`sideEffectTempVars` hoist (~2279):** collapsed the `.map { ... single-decl VariableStatement }` into a single `VariableStatement` with `declarations = sideEffectTempVars.map { ... }` so multiple temps render as `var _a, _b, _c, _d;`. Matches TypeScript's emit style (most numeric-temp baselines use comma-joined `var` declarations).

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1343/3 (was 10078/1344/3, +1 net). Zero regressions. The `var _a, _b, ...` collapse is conservative — the only call sites adding to `sideEffectTempVars` are the empty-destructuring side-effect paths (one path adds 1 temp per `export const {} = ...`; the new `walkArrayPattern` adds more for nested empty arrays). Collapsing them all into one var statement matches TypeScript's invariant that consecutive synthetic temp-var declarations are comma-joined.

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** The JS-emit pool still has dozens of tractable candidates surfaced by the one-off ranker; `find_candidates.py` doesn't see them because it only mines `.errors.txt` diffs. The next agent should re-run the ranker with `/tmp/find_js_emit_candidates.py`. Other 2-4 line diffs that remain in the pool include: `dontShowCompilerGeneratedMembers_ts` (`-;` `;` parser leak — see B19.2 skip note), `parseJsxElementInUnaryExpressionNoCrash1_ts` (JSX-in-`.js` w/ `allowJs` — historical decision), `parserUnparsedTokenCrash2_ts` (paren placement for `==== ` — deep parser), `classMemberWithMissingIdentifier2_ts` (`[name:string]` recovery as `[name, string]` — colon-as-comma in array literal), and `nestedGlobalNamespaceInClass_ts` (`global x` inside class triggers namespace-IIFE emit — multi-level recovery).

**Session 2026-05-17 (B23.1, +1, 8730 → 8731 passing — flips `optionalChainWithInstantiationExpression2_ts__target_es2019` JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B22.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3/62/12). Recent commit log healthy (B17.x → B22.1 stack — no recon-only pattern). Anti-loop rule satisfied. Re-ran the B21.1/B22.1 JS-emit ranker (`/tmp/rank_js_emit.py`) — top tractable JS-emit-only candidates: `optionalChainWithInstantiationExpression2_ts` (3-line diff, instantiation-expr + optional-chain interaction). The other shortlisted candidates were ruled out: `asyncArrowInClassES5_ts` (static-arrow `_a = Test` capture pattern — niche, unclear root cause), `ClassDeclaration26_ts` (TypeScript invents `() => {}` from malformed `var constructor() { }` — multi-level recovery), `moduleResolutionWithRequire_ts` (suppress emit of file imported only via runtime `require()` — risky), `reactReduxLikeDeferredInferenceAllowsAssignment_ts` / `mappedTypeGenericIndexedAccess_ts` (per B22.1 — too broad), `nodeNextImportModeImplicitIndexResolution_ts` (needs package.json `"type"` plumbing — cross-cutting), `dontShowCompilerGeneratedMembers_ts` (parser-recovery would have to leak `-` past B19.2's consume-to-`}` — non-trivial), `parseJsxElementInUnaryExpressionNoCrash1_ts` (`.js` file with `allowJs` parsed as JSX — historical decision; flipping risks regressing JSX-in-`.js` corpus), `experimentalDecoratorMetadataUnresolvedTypeObjectInEmit_ts` (chained `_a/_b/_c/_d` decorator metadata safety wrap for deeply-nested qualified name — distinct multi-temp-var sequence emission, deferred per B21.1 notes).

  **Root cause.** Source (the failing sub-test is `target=es2019`): `declare const a: A | undefined; a<b>?.();`. `a<b>` is an instantiation expression (TypeScript 4.7+); the type args are erased at emit. Expected ES2019 output: `(_a = (a)) === null || _a === void 0 ? void 0 : _a();` — the optional call `?.()` downlevel captures the LHS in `_a` and parens `(a)` are preserved. Our previous behavior: TS1477 ("instantiation expression cannot be followed by a property access") fires unconditionally for `?.` after type args because the parser doesn't distinguish optional CALL (`?.(`)/INDEX (`?.[`) from optional PROPERTY ACCESS (`?.IDENTIFIER`). The wrong TS1477 emit was silent — no `errors.txt` baseline exists for this test — but the resulting parse tree was `CallExpression(a, questionDot=true)` with bare-Identifier LHS, so the downlevel optimization for bare-Identifier `a` applied and emitted `a === null || a === void 0 ? void 0 : a();` (no temp var, no parens).

  **Two-piece fix.**
  - **Parser.kt:** In the LessThan postfix-loop branch's `canFollowTypeArgumentsInExpression()` sub-case, when `token == QuestionDot`, peek one more token via `scanner.lookAhead { scanner.scan(); scanner.getToken() }`. If the token after `?.` is `(` or `[`, it's an optional call/index — wrap `result` in a synthetic `ParenthesizedExpression(instantiationEnd = typeArgsEnd)` and continue the postfix loop (which then consumes `?.()` via the existing `QuestionDot+OpenParen` case at line ~4307, producing `CallExpression(ParenthesizedExpression(a), questionDot=true)`). If the next token is anything else (identifier → property access), keep the existing TS1477 + optional-chain-paren-wrap behavior unchanged.
  - **Transformer.kt:** In the CallExpression branch's non-downlevel (ES2020+) path, when `expr.questionDotToken == true` AND `transformedExpr is ParenthesizedExpression && transformedExpr.instantiationEnd != null && transformedExpr.expression is Identifier`, strip the synthetic paren so ES2020 emits bare `a?.()`. The downlevel (ES2019-) path already routes `transformedExpr is ParenthesizedExpression` into the temp-var branch (line 7163 check `if (transformedExpr is Identifier)` is false), so no change needed there.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1344/3 (was 10078/1345/3, +1 net). Both `optionalChainWithInstantiationExpression2_ts` sub-tests (ES2019 and ES2020) pass; the ES2020 case was already passing pre-change and remained passing because the transformer strip preserves its bare-`a?.()` emit. Zero regressions.

  **Risk profile that materialized.** Bounded as projected. The lookahead gate fires only when (a) type args parsed successfully via `tryParseTypeArguments` (returns from `scanner.tryScan` only on success), (b) `canFollowTypeArgumentsInExpression()` returns true (already a tight gate — only specific operator/punctuation tokens), (c) token is exactly `QuestionDot`, (d) the token after `?.` is `(` or `[`. All four conditions together select instantiation-expr-then-optional-call/index sites only. The transformer strip's gate is even tighter: synthetic paren (`instantiationEnd != null`), wrapping an Identifier, on the LHS of an optional-call CallExpression with target >= ES2020. Did NOT extend the analogous transformer strip to ElementAccessExpression's non-downlevel branch — no failing test demands it and adding it risks regressing some path-aware comment/paren preservation case.

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** The remaining JS-emit ranker hits include `mappedTypeGenericIndexedAccess_ts` and `reactReduxLikeDeferredInferenceAllowsAssignment_ts` — both gated on broader async/downlevel rework. `experimentalDecoratorMetadataUnresolvedTypeObjectInEmit_ts` (chained `_a/_b/_c/_d` decorator metadata wrap for deeply-nested type ref) is still the most promising next +1 in the JS-emit pool but requires multi-temp-var sequence emission (distinct from B21.1's single-temp shape). `nodeNextImportModeImplicitIndexResolution_ts` needs package.json `"type": "module"` plumbing — moderate scope (CompilerOptions has no place for per-package context yet; would need to thread a map from package.json paths to `"type"` values through the parse pipeline).

**Session 2026-05-17 (B22.1, +2, 8728 → 8730 passing — flips `es6ImportParseErrors_ts` errors-baseline + JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B21.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3/62/12). Recent commit log healthy (B17.x → B21.1 stack — no recon-only pattern). Anti-loop rule satisfied. Reused the B21.1 JS-emit ranker technique (`/tmp/rank_js_emit3.py` — handles self-closing `<testcase/>` correctly). Top candidates surfaced: `parserUnparsedTokenCrash2_ts` (paren placement, deep parser issue), `mappedTypeGenericIndexedAccess_ts` (needs `.call`-preserving optional-call downlevel + `var _a` arrow-wrap — too broad), `reactReduxLikeDeferredInferenceAllowsAssignment_ts` (async destructured-param flattening — too broad), `es6ImportParseErrors_ts` (parser recovery — tractable).

  **Root cause.** Source: `// @target: es6\nimport 10;`. TypeScript's expected behavior: TS1128 "Declaration or statement expected." at the `import` keyword (length 6) AND JS emit `"use strict"; 10;` — the parser treats `import` followed by a numeric literal as an unrecognizable shape, emits TS1128, skips only the `import` keyword, and continues parsing `10;` as ExpressionStatement. Our previous behavior: fell into `parseImportDeclaration` → `parseImportClause` → `parseNamedImports` and emitted TS1005 `'{' expected.` at the `10`, TS1005 `'}' expected.`, and TS2307 "Cannot find module ';'" — a 3-error cascade. The malformed ImportDeclaration also made `isModuleFile` return true so the emitter produced `export {};` instead of the expected `"use strict"; 10;`.

  **Single-file fix in Parser.kt (~452).** In the `ImportKeyword` branch of `parseStatement`, add a third lookahead alongside the existing `nextIsParen` / `nextIsDot` checks: `nextIsInvalid` peeks the token after `import` and returns true for `NumericLiteral`, `BigIntLiteral`, `RegularExpressionLiteral` — none of which can start a valid import clause (StringLiteral for side-effect imports, Identifier for default name, OpenBrace for named, Asterisk for namespace, TypeKeyword for type-only). When `nextIsInvalid`, emit TS1128 "Declaration or statement expected." at `import` (default `getTokenPos()`, length 6), call `nextToken()` to consume only the keyword, and return null. The outer `parseStatements` loop sees that progress was made (token advanced) and that stmt is null, then re-enters and parses `10;` as ExpressionStatement.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1345/3 (was 10078/1347/3, +2 net). Zero regressions. Both `es6ImportParseErrors_ts` sub-tests flip clean.

  **Risk profile that materialized.** Bounded as projected. The lookahead gate fires ONLY when (a) the statement-start token is `import`, AND (b) the next token is exclusively `NumericLiteral` / `BigIntLiteral` / `RegularExpressionLiteral`. All valid import forms (string-literal side-effect, identifier default, named, namespace, type-only) keep the original `parseImportDeclaration` path. The conservative literal-token set avoids false-positive recovery for shapes that the existing parseImportDeclaration error path handles correctly (e.g. malformed identifier names, missing commas, etc.).

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** Same recovery shape could be extended to other clearly-invalid post-`import` tokens (e.g. operators like `+`, `-`, statement-terminators like `;` immediately after `import`) — but no failing test currently demands it. The B21.1 JS-emit ranker (`/tmp/rank_js_emit3.py`) still has tractable candidates: `parseJsxElementInUnaryExpressionNoCrash1_ts` (3-line diff, parser-recovery for JSX), `experimentalDecoratorMetadataUnresolvedTypeObjectInEmit_ts` (B21.1 mentioned as follow-on — chained `_a/_b/_c/_d` wrap for deeply-nested type refs), `classMemberWithMissingIdentifier2_ts` (computed-property recovery), `ambiguousGenericAssertion1_ts` (cast-vs-generic-fn parse).

**Session 2026-05-17 (B21.1, +1, 8727 → 8728 passing — flips `decoratorMetadataNoLibIsolatedModulesTypes_ts` JS-emit sub-test).** Continuation /loop iteration after B20.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3/62/12). Wrote a one-off scanner that ranks JS-emit failures by minus+plus line count; top candidate was `decoratorMetadataNoLibIsolatedModulesTypes_ts` (3-line diff) — a known-pattern fix (decorator-metadata safety wrapper) extending the existing `wrapDefaultImportMetadataArgsInExpr` mechanism.

  **Root cause.** Source: `@noLib: true; @isolatedModules: true; @emitDecoratorMetadata: true; class B { @Decorate member: Map<string, number>; }`. Currently we emit `__metadata("design:type", Map)` — bare identifier. Expected: `var _a; ... __metadata("design:type", typeof (_a = typeof Map !== "undefined" && Map) === "function" ? _a : Object)`. TypeScript safety-wraps when the type reference's name may not exist at runtime (under noLib, lib globals like Map aren't loaded; under isolatedModules, cross-file types can't be verified).

  **Single-file fix in Transformer.kt:** Added new helpers `wrapNoLibMetadataArgsInStatement` / `wrapNoLibMetadataArgsInExpr` / `wrapNoLibIdentifier` mirroring the existing `wrapDefaultImportMetadataArgsInExpr` shape. The wrap fires for bare Identifier args matching a curated `NOLIB_RUNTIME_QUESTIONABLE` set (Map, Set, WeakMap, WeakSet, Promise, Symbol, BigInt, Proxy, Reflect, ArrayBuffer, SharedArrayBuffer, DataView, *Int*Array, *Float*Array, BigInt64Array, BigUint64Array) — the common ES library globals likely to be polyfilled. Post-process inserted at the end of `transformPhase3`, **NOT inside `transformToCommonJS`** — the test uses ESM output (target=es2015 default module=es2015), so a CJS-scoped fix would have missed it. `var _a;` insertion placed after RawStatement (helpers) but before other statements.

  **First-attempt gotcha (recorded for future agents).** Placed the post-process inside `transformToCommonJS` initially — built clean but the test still failed with the same diff (no `var _a;` injected). The println debug showed the code path was never entered, because `transformToCommonJS` only runs when `useCJS` is true (module:commonjs / none / NodeNext / .cts/.cjs file). For ESM/AMD/UMD/System output, those module-specific transformers run and return early — bypassing the CJS-local post-processes. Lesson: when adding a transform that should apply across ALL module formats, place it AFTER the module-format dispatch in `transformPhase3` (just before the final `return sourceFile.copy(statements = ...)` at the bottom), NOT inside any one module-transform helper.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1347/3 (was 10078/1348/3, +1 net). Zero regressions. Target test `decoratorMetadataNoLibIsolatedModulesTypes_ts` JS-emit sub-test flips clean (errors-baseline sub-test was already failing on TS2583 missing — separate root cause, requires lib-version-aware error suggestions).

  **Risk profile that materialized.** Bounded as projected. The gate (`noLib && isolatedModules && needsMetadataHelper`) only fires for one test in the corpus (verified via `grep -rln "@noLib: true" tests/cases/compiler/ | xargs grep -l "emitDecoratorMetadata"` returning only the target). The curated identifier-set restricts wrapping to ES library globals — user-defined classes / interfaces / type aliases don't match. `_a` naming collision risk is bounded (existing `_a` usage is gated on `defaultModuleTempVars.isNotEmpty()` for the CJS-only default-import wrap, which can't co-occur with ESM output of the same file).

Not a simple extension of B21.1 — needs distinct multi-temp-var sequence emission.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B20.1, +2, 8725 → 8727 passing — flips `assertInWrapSomeTypeParameter_ts` errors-baseline + JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B19.2. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3 EXTRA / 62 MISSING / 12 SWAP); recent commit log shows healthy feature work (B17.x / B18.x / B19.x stack — no recon-only pattern). Anti-loop rule satisfied. Surveyed the skip-log + recent session notes for tractable parser-recovery candidates from the B17.x "Recon outcome" list. Picked `assertInWrapSomeTypeParameter_ts` — was unlisted in the skip-log; B17.x recon noted it as one of "remaining +1/+2 emit candidates" requiring multi-piece recovery, but on closer inspection it turned out to be a single-piece fix.

  **Root cause.** Source: `foo<U extends C<C<T>>(x: U)` — one `>` short of being a valid generic method header. TypeScript's baseline emits a single TS1005 `'>' expected.` at the `(` position. Our parser produced an 8-error cascade and emitted `>>(x)` as the method name in the JS output. Trace: in `tryParseTypeParameters`, we successfully parsed `U extends C<C<T>>` as a `TypeParameter` (the inner `C<C<T>>` parses fine because `tryParseTypeArguments` already has `reScanGreaterToken` to split `>>`). After the loop exits (no comma), we check `if (token != GreaterThan)` and see `(` — fall through to `return null`, tryScan rolls back to the original `<`. Then `parseParameterList` calls `parseExpected(OpenParen)` at the still-pending `<`, emitting TS1005 `(` expected. — but worse, the cascade continues as the parser mis-interprets `<U extends C<C<T>>` as parameter shape (with `<` as an identifier-looking start), producing TS2391 / TS2300 / TS2304 noise.

  **Single-piece fix in Parser.kt:** in `tryParseTypeParameters`, after the params-collection loop and BEFORE the existing `if (token != GreaterThan) return null` branch, add a recovery check: when `token == OpenParen && params.isNotEmpty()`, emit TS1005 `'>' expected.` at the `(` position (length 1) and return the parsed params (no rollback). The caller's `parseParameterList()` then proceeds at `(` normally.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1348/3 (was 10078/1350/3, +2 net). Zero regressions. Both `assertInWrapSomeTypeParameter_ts` sub-tests flip clean.

  **Risk profile that materialized.** Bounded as projected. The recovery fires only when ALL of: (a) `<` was consumed (we're inside the typeParam list), (b) at least one valid TypeParameter was parsed, (c) the next non-typeParam-continuation token is `(`. Condition (c) — `OpenParen` — is the dominant follow-on for generic methods/functions/constructors (~11 of the 16 callers pair `parseTypeParametersOpt` with `parseParameterList`). Other callers (interface-decl, type-alias-decl, class-decl) follow `parseTypeParametersOpt` with `parseHeritageClauses`/`Equals`/`OpenBrace`, where `(` would not appear — so this recovery would never fire spuriously in those contexts. Well-formed `foo<U>(x)` paths are unaffected (token is `>` after parsing `U`, never `(`).

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** The same recovery shape could be extended to other "missing closer" cases (e.g. `<U,` followed by EOF or `)`), but no failing test currently demands it. The recovery is bounded enough to extend opportunistically if a similar candidate surfaces in future recon.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B19.2, +1, 8724 → 8725 passing — flips `dontShowCompilerGeneratedMembers_ts` errors-baseline sub-test).** Continuation /loop iteration after B19.1. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3 EXTRA / 62 MISSING / 12 SWAP); recent commit log shows no empty-pool refresh pattern (all recent commits are real feature work — B17.x, B18.x, B19.1 stack). Anti-loop rule satisfied. Surveyed the skip-log for tractable parser-recovery candidates from the "Recon outcome" list under B17.1–B17.9 session notes; picked `dontShowCompilerGeneratedMembers_ts` (was unlisted in the skip-log; +1 errors-baseline sub-test in `find_candidates.py`'s unfiltered output).

  **Root cause.** Source: `var f: { x: number; <- };`. The `<-` shape inside the type literal hits `parseTypeMember`'s `LessThan` branch (call-signature with type params). `parseTypeParametersOpt()` is speculative (uses `scanner.tryScan`) — when `parseTypeParameter` returns null at line 5437 (`!isIdentifier()` because `-` isn't a valid identifier), tryScan restores state and parseTypeParametersOpt returns null. Then `parseParameterList()` calls `parseExpected(OpenParen)` at the still-pending `<`, emitting TS1005 `'(' expected.` (wrong position, wrong code). TypeScript's baseline expects TS1139 "Type parameter declaration expected." at the `-` AND TS1109 "Expression expected." at the trailing `}`.

  **Single-piece fix in Parser.kt (~2105).** Pre-check at the top of the `LessThan` branch in `parseTypeMember`: `scanner.lookAhead { scanner.scan(); ... }` peeks the token immediately after `<` (without committing); if it is NOT a valid identifier / modifier (`const`/`in`/`out`) / `>` start, treat it as a malformed type-parameter list. (a) Consume `<` via `nextToken()`. (b) Emit TS1139 at `scanner.getTokenPos()` length 1 (the offending non-identifier token). (c) Consume tokens until member terminator (`}` / `;` / `,` / EOF) — prevents leakage into the outer statement parser. (d) When terminator is `}`, emit TS1109 at the close-brace position to match TypeScript's "stranded close-brace at expression-expected position" behavior. (e) Return null from `parseTypeMember` so the outer member loop proceeds cleanly to `parseExpected(CloseBrace)`.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1350/3 (was 10078/1351/3, +1 net). Zero regressions. The `dontShowCompilerGeneratedMembers_ts` errors-baseline sub-test now passes with all 2 diagnostics in correct positions (TS1139 at 3,6 length 1; TS1109 at 4,1 length 1). The JS-emit sub-test still fails on a separate upstream `-;` and `;` leakage that my change doesn't address — out of scope (a different parser path is producing those statements; likely a pre-existing issue with how the outer parser recovers when the variable declaration's type annotation is partially malformed).

  **Risk profile that materialized.** Bounded as projected. The lookahead gate fires ONLY when (a) token is `<` in `parseTypeMember`'s call-signature branch AND (b) the next token is non-identifier / non-modifier / non-`>`. Well-formed `<T extends ...>` and `<const T>` and `<>` (empty) keep the prior speculative path. The recovery-skip loop terminates on `}`/`;`/`,`/EOF — bounded scope.

  **Foundation for follow-ons.** Same lookahead gate could be added to `parseTypeMember`'s `NewKeyword` branch (line 2121) for `new <-` shapes — but no failing test currently demands it. The `parseTypeMember` LessThan branch is also exercised by similar candidates in the recon list (`parserUnparsedTokenCrash2_ts`, `ambiguousGenericAssertion1_ts`) — worth re-checking after this lands whether any other tests flip from the same change.

**Session 2026-05-17 (B19.1, +2, 8722 → 8724 passing — flips `parseJsxElementInUnaryExpressionNoCrash3_ts` errors-baseline + JS-emit).** Continuation /loop iteration after B18.4. `find_candidates.py --fresh` returned 0/0/0 (filtered from 3 EXTRA / 62 MISSING / 12 SWAP). Spot-checked the EXTRA and SWAP buckets — all confirmed as Blocker #2/#3/#4 architectural per skip-log. Ran MAINT-1-style stale-skip audit (proper section boundary this time — the prior MAINT-1 was thorough; only ~0 stale entries surfaced). With the surgical pool genuinely exhausted, picked the `parseJsxElementInUnaryExpressionNoCrash3_ts` target from B10.1's "Foundation for follow-ons" — flagged as needing different span/tag-name extraction for the malformed `!< {:>` shape.

  **Root cause.** For source `!< {:>`: after `<` is consumed, the next token is `{` which is NOT a valid JSX tag-name-start. The existing `parseJsxTagName` unconditionally takes any token's text as the tag name, so it consumed `{` as tagName="{". Subsequent attribute parsing consumed `:` as a boolean attribute, and the `>` closed the opening tag. Result: TS17008 emitted with tag name `{` at wrong position (1,4) instead of empty-string at (1,3); no TS1003, no inner TS1005.

  **Three-piece fix in Parser.kt:**
  - **`parseJsxElementOrFragmentBody` (Parser.kt ~3682):** added a new pre-check before `parseJsxTagName()`. When `token == SyntaxKind.OpenBrace`, emit TS1003 "Identifier expected." at the `{` position (default scanner.getTokenPos()), construct `Identifier(text = "", pos = scanner.getPrevTokenEnd(), end = ...+1)` as the synthetic tag name, and DO NOT consume `{`. The leftover `{` then gets handled by `parseJsxAttributes` → `parseJsxAttribute` as a (malformed) spread attribute.
  - **`parseJsxAttribute` (Parser.kt ~3850):** changed the spread-attribute branch to gate on the `parseExpected(DotDotDot)` return value. When it returns false (the malformed-spread case `{:` where `:` is where `...` should be), skip tokens until `}`, `/`, `>`, or EOF — do NOT call `parseAssignmentExpression`. The prior behavior called the expression parser on whatever followed `{`, which consumed `:` and the closing `>` of the opening tag as part of an expression, producing wrong JS emit `!< {...} :>` instead of `!< {...}>`. The recovery uses an empty-text `Identifier` as the spread expression — the emitter renders `{...}` literally for empty-text expressions.
  - **`tokenToString` (Parser.kt ~6566):** added `DotDotDot -> "..."` so TS1005's `'...' expected.` message reads correctly. Previously it fell through to `else -> kind.name` and produced `'DotDotDot' expected.`.

  **Verification.** Full-suite 10078/1351/3 (was 10078/1353/3, +2 net). Zero regressions. Both target sub-tests flip clean: errors-baseline (4 emissions in correct order — TS17008 at (1,3) empty tag, TS1003 at (1,4), TS1005 `'...' expected.` at (1,5), TS1005 `'</' expected.` at (3,1)) and JS-emit (`!< {...}>` + `</>;`).

  **Risk profile that materialized.** Bounded as projected. The `<{` tag-name guard fires only when the IMMEDIATE post-`<` token is `OpenBrace` — a malformed input pattern. The spread-attribute recovery only takes the new path when `parseExpected(DotDotDot)` fails (the missing-`...` shape) — properly-formed `{...expr}` spread attributes are unchanged. The `tokenToString` extension is purely additive (other call sites already had explicit message text). The `coerceAtLeast(1)` for TS17008 squiggle length that I added during exploration was reverted — TypeScript's baseline expects a zero-length squiggle for empty tag names (no `~` in the source-echo squiggle line).

  **Foundation for follow-ons:** The B11.1 fragment-EOF code in `parseJsxElementOrFragmentBody` could be extended to detect mid-source unclosed cases (B10.3 candidate), but no failing test currently demands it. The `<{` recovery established here also handles future malformed-input tests of the same shape — should one surface in the corpus.

more common in class-shape AST recovery — so the same lookahead gate that worked for
interfaces over-fired against legitimate class-body shapes.

| Feature | Diagnostic codes unblocked | "None produced" test impact |
|---|---|---|
| Contextual typing | TS2322 (1267), TS2345 (576), TS2353 (110) | **~600+ tests** |

| Overload resolution | TS2769 (511), TS2349 (161) | **~200+ tests** |

These are UPPER bounds — a test usually needs multiple features. Realistic gain per feature: 30-50% of the upper bound.

- [x] **B83.4e (LANDED 2026-05-27 round 52 iter12, net-zero infra): Union-property-of-Object synthesis for primitive-union receivers in `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess`.** Iter11 instrumentation confirmed B83.4d-quick's push mechanism works end-to-end: `currentLocalTypes[x] = string | number` is set, `getTypeOfIdentifier(x)` returns it correctly, but `getTypeOfPropertyAccess(x.toString)` (the most common failing-test body shape) returns `any` because `getPropertyOfType` on a Union of primitives doesn't synthesize the common member. **Implementation surface**: in `getPropertyOfType` (search Checker.kt), when receiver is `Type.Union` of primitives (e.g. `string | number`), look up the property on each constituent's apparent type (`String` wrapper, `Number` wrapper), and if ALL constituents resolve the property to compatible types, return the union of those property types (or a synthesized common type). Concrete bench: `(string | number).toString()` should return `string` (both `String.toString()` and `Number.toString()` return `string`). **Risk**: MEDIUM — broader narrowing through union members affects many tests beyond B83.4d-quick targets; should be gated to `Type.Union` where ALL constituents are primitives (not anonymous-Object / Reference / Interface members — those have orthogonal property-resolution semantics). **Target tests for follow-through validation**: `mismatchedExplicitTypeParameterAndArgumentType_ts`, `inferentialTypingWithFunctionType*`, the 6 candidates surveyed in iter11 recon. **Backpointer**: closes the iter11-identified blocker for B83.4d-quick to actually flip tests rather than remain net-zero infra.
